ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U1	Good afternoon, honourable Riigikogu. Good afternoon, Prime Minister and Minister. Let's start the briefing. First, please, let's do the presence check! 32 members of the Riigikogu registered as present, 69 are missing. The following members of government will attend today's Question Time: Prime Minister Andrus Ansip, Minister of Defence Urmas Reinsalu and Minister of Justice Hanno Pevkur.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U2	Ladies and gentlemen, since five issues have been previously registered, there are only two issues that can be put forward today for each subject on the spot. Let's get to the first question. Please, colleague Priit Toobal! The question is for Prime Minister Andrus Ansip.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U3	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Prime Minister! Next year, the second term of office of Estonian Euro Commissioner Siim Kallas will end and Estonia will have to appoint a new Euro Commissioner. When is the government going to take this decision, and has the government partner been agreed on this?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U4	Thank you. The decision will be made when the time is right. Until now, we have not been negotiating with a government partner. This issue has not been addressed at government level.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U5	Please, Mr Toobal, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U6	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Good Prime Minister! In Estonia, the Government of the Republic appoints the Euro Commissioner. But perhaps you can give examples from other countries of the European Union. In other countries, is it within the competence of the government, or is this Commissioner appointed by Parliament on some sides, for example, or does Parliament or some committee need coordination? How does this work in other parts of Europe?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U7	Thank you. I knew that you could ask this question, and that is why I have provided myself with information from Finland. A fairly thorough study has been carried out and it has been found that in most Member States of the European Union it is the government's responsibility to appoint a Commissioner for the European Commission. According to the success queen, there is only one country in the European Union where the government's proposal is voted by the full chamber of Parliament. This country is Lithuania. No other country will vote on the candidate for Commissioner of the European Commission in Parliament. There are also countries where this candidate is heard in the Committee on European Union Affairs, but most of the Member States of the European Union have exclusive competence to appoint a Commissioner for the Commission.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U8	Please, Mr Toobal, the second point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U9	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Good Prime Minister! You said the government hasn't discussed this issue yet, but there's not much time left to make that decision, just a few months. I ask what your personal position and desire is. Do you want to become an Estonian Euro Commissioner?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U10	Thank you. I have once answered this question very correctly. The answer was that I have not been able to think of this profession as tempting. But I fully understand that whether I think the profession is attractive and offers me enough self-realization or not is not the most important thing. More importantly, the government would call me a candidate if I had that wish. Secondly, the President of the European Commission, who has been authorised to set up the European Commission, may alone reject one or the other nomination. Thirdly, according to the Treaty of Lisbon, the European Parliament is the parliamentary institution that screens the candidates, and it can also reject some nominations. So there are a lot of decision-makers, regardless of whether we're talking about candidate A, B or C. That's why I don't even bother to speculate on all possible scenarios.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U11	Now two issues registered on the spot. Please, colleague Heimar Lenk!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U12	Thank you. Prime Minister! Your candidacy in Europe would hardly be rejected, after all, you have been a representative of Europe's fine child, of the Republic of Estonia, and always received praise for our progress. But can you tell us which parameters are used to make this choice, whether the individual characteristics of a person? Where does the government come from when he decides to choose this, the second or the third man? What kind of person does that have to be?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U13	Article 17 (3) of the Treaty on European Union states: 'The members of the Commission shall be chosen on the basis of their general expertise and their commitment to European affairs from persons whose independence is beyond doubt. The Commission shall be completely independent in the performance of its duties. Without prejudice to Article 18 (2), the members of the Commission shall neither seek nor receive instructions from any government, institution, body, office or agency. They shall refrain from any action incompatible with their duties or with the performance of their duties. ' This is enshrined in the Treaty. Obviously, a potential candidate must meet these conditions.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U14	Please, the last issue registered on the ground, Mr Simson!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U15	Thank you. The previous questioner asked why the government decided to choose this or another man. The European Commission is aware that it is very important that there are also women among the Commissioners. There are now a third of women among them. On this basis, it can be assumed that if Estonia has been represented for ten years by a male top politician, we are now expected to have a completely different approach. Has there been any message to the government that this time Estonia should send a very competent woman to represent itself?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U16	Obviously, it is not the government's priority what gender a potential candidate is. ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U17	Thank you very much, Prime Minister. I will conclude on this issue.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U18	The next question is my colleague, Marianne Mikkolt, to Mr Reinsalu, Minister for Defence. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U19	Thank you very much, Mr President. Prime Minister and ministers! This year, 15 years passed from the time a government decree set up the United Educational Institutions of the Defence Forces. This is a fully respectable higher education institution in the Estonian era of independence, the history of which dates back to 1919. Thus, the Defence Forces United Educational Institutions are the only militaryly organised state-defence university of applied sciences located in the area of government of the Ministry of Defence of the Republic of Estonia and under the authority of the Commander of the Defence Forces. This educational institution is the promoter of military research and development in the Republic of Estonia. Students will receive a decent scholarship and receive uniforms. But why does this university of applied sciences have problems filling places every year? Not all places were filled this year either. Secretary of Defense Reinsalu, why do you think this is the case?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U20	Thank you. Honourable questioner! Defence United Educational Institutions, including the Higher Military School, where officers are trained as well as the Battle School, which provides vocational training, will certainly play a very important role in ensuring the quality of our national defence. It is also very important to clarify that the best possible candidates consider the position of a professional military officer. Young people studying in upper secondary schools should be aware of the opportunities offered, including that the profession of a military officer is stable and provides a solid income. The wage reform carried out in the Defence Forces in the spring of this year is very important in this respect. We saw the impact of this already last year, when it was known that wage reform would come. Then more people came into the military than they left. There was no such positive trend for several years. It is important to us that competition in higher military schools is increased. Greater competition is one indicator that among young people the profession of an officer, the profession of a professional military officer, is more valued. In 2013, 59 young people began to study. 125 young people came to the entrance tests, 122 remaining after the general physical test, 122 candidates after writing the Estonian essay were also 122 and 79 after the qualifying interview. So there is, in fact, an effective sieve, but certainly, in my opinion, competition could be stronger.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U21	Please, Mr Mikko, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U22	If the Republic of Estonia had an officer to be proud and good, there would be a major competition for both the Higher Military School and the Battle School. Unfortunately, no such storm or urge has been observed in Tartu. How can we explain the relative unpopularity of the sub-office and what should we do to ensure that young people come to the United Defence Study Institutions so that there is a valid competition for positions? What can I do?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U23	Thank you. I think that the profession of officer and sub-officer, the profession of professional military officer at all (there are also more occupations and professions) is certainly more than just a paid job. A person chooses this profession for life. The Defence Forces are built on a career system. This means that it is assumed that the Defence Forces will be able to use these people and their experiences long enough. Motivative income, self-realisation opportunities and a clear human resources policy are certainly very important. I stressed that this spring, there was a wage reform in the Defence Forces. With regard to the personnel policy of the Defence Forces, the Commander of the Defence Forces will develop clear guidelines and the military part of the defence development plan established for ten years should give professional defence forces an understanding of the type of defence force we will have in the ten-year term and on the basis of what priorities it will develop. All of this helps young capable people to understand that the position of a Defence Forcesman is stable, provides income for the Defence Forces and their families, and it is possible for the Defence Forces to test themselves both in Estonia and elsewhere. But in addition to this, the ethical, moral dimension is of course important when choosing an officer's profession, the development of which certainly also plays a major role in the practical work of the Defence Forces.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U24	Please, my colleague Marianne Mikko, the second point of clarification.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U25	Thank you. There have been times when the surplus of the money of the Defence Forces United Educational Institutions reached even 300 million Estonian kroons at the end of the year. What is the surplus, Mr Minister, in this year, in 2013 and where will these amounts be spent in this few and a half months?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U26	Thank you. When we talk about the general budget of the Defence Forces, it should be 100% as it stands today. There is, of course, a role here for the current management costs, but also for the investment payments that are being made. In this regard, some previous years have shown problems with the implementation of the budget. They have not so much resulted from weak planning as from the fact that there have been problems with individual large-scale procurement. There have been challenges to procurement and problems regarding the failure of the winner of the major procurement contest to meet the deadline and therefore it has not been considered appropriate to pay out the money. As regards the budget and implementation of the Joint Training Institutions of the Defence Forces, this budget, too, is expected to be implemented on an ongoing basis by the end of this year.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U27	Two locally registered questions can now be submitted. Please, my colleague, Urbo Vaarmann!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U28	Thank you. Mr. Secretary of Defense! I'm asking about motivation. I hear military personnel are paying rents in dormitories right now. There wasn't one before. Aren't you afraid there'll be a situation where dorms will be empty?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U29	Thank you. The career of a frame defence officer also means rotation over the course of his career. In order to alleviate the problems, the housing of the frame defence forces has been established, there are official apartments, as well as dorms. My guideline has been that these surfaces should be accessible only to persons who are entitled to use them, i.e. frame defence officers, officers or sub-officers. There is no projected surge in spending here. Therefore, given the current cost of using office housing, which is 32 cents per square metre, it should certainly not be the case that business apartments are not to be used. On the contrary, there's a queue to get a dwelling. These apartments are distributed by the Defence Forces themselves on the basis of a Commission decision. I am sure that this will be done transparently and that only those who have the right to do so will have access to housing.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U30	The last issue registered on the ground, Mr Mati Raidma, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U31	Thank you very much. Dear Minister, I return to the question of how popular this school is among young people. Do you see any role here in the school's national defence education and the idea of some schools to develop it further in the field of in-depth education? What thoughts and ideas do the Department of Defense have on this for next year?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U32	Thank you. That is an important question. These issues are certainly linked. I've met the cadets myself and asked them how they had the idea of going to a higher military school. A lot of them have responded to me that they got this idea from their national defense teacher as part of their upper secondary school course, some have said they were just coming to see. There are those to whom this has been a highly aware professional choice, and there are also those who have chosen between several civil professions and the military profession. I will not show modesty about learning conditions. The economic conditions in this school are certainly very good: scholarship, free living environment, etc. If, over the next four years, we are able to implement the national defence education programme currently being developed by the Ministry of Defence in such a way that all students in all general education schools receive this education, it would certainly improve significantly, I have no doubt, the motivation of young people to consider, among other things, the profession of a professional defence officer.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U33	Thank you very much, Mr. Secretary of Defense. I will conclude on this issue.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U34	The next question is Mr Andrus Ansip, my colleague, Mr Ester Tuksoo, to Prime Minister. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U35	Thank you very much, Madam President. Prime Minister! Yesterday's Eesti Päevaleht read that the Ministry of the Interior has given the management of the waiting area of the Koidula border crossing point to the offshore company Paldiski Sadamate AS. The offshore area is mostly used for non-payment of taxes for domestic business. My colleague Sõerd has also condemned this. What kind of signal does the government give to society with this step?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U36	Thank you. I also read this article on the Estonian Päevaleht. Maybe I'm wrong now, but I didn't count it as an offshore company. In my opinion, this was a company registered in Estonia, owned by an offshore company. There is a significant difference between whether the service is provided by an offshore company or is the owner, shareholder, shareholder, offshore company of the service company. The state has announced a procurement, a transparent open competition. Offers have been submitted to the open competition and the best one has won. I'm not familiar with their particular procurement circumstances, I guess I don't have to be. However, I think that simply because the owner is an offshore company, the company registered in Estonia does not have to assume any maliciousness immediately. I would approach the question in good faith.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U37	Please, Mrs Tuiksoo, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U38	Thank you, Madam President. Sometimes you have to approach in good faith. In the middle of last year, however, Chancellor Teder took the view that if a person has previously booked and paid for the vehicle in the queue on the Internet, the fee for using the waiting area may not be charged if the vehicle is immediately diverted from the waiting area to the border crossing point. Teder sent a proposal to the Minister for the Interior to eliminate the unlawfulness of charging for the use of waiting areas at the border crossing points in Koidula and Luhamaa. Why has the Minister of the Interior in your government not yet fulfilled the proposal of the Chancellor of Justice?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U39	Thank you. The waiting list system for border crossings consists of two components: the electronic reservation fee for border crossings and the fee for the specific services provided in the waiting area. On this point, I share the opinion of the Chancellor of Justice. If this waiting area is not actually used, it is not appropriate to charge for it. The actual circumstances led to why we created an electronic queue for border crossings and a parking complex for residential services. There was a screaming need for it. There was a time when the border line was 50 kilometres long and the border had to be waited for a week, for example, in the direction of Narva. It was never possible for a simple person to cross the border. Traffic-dangerous situations arose, as cars took the lane, nature was polluted, etc. This was not only an Estonian question, but it has been exactly the same in other countries. In Valalimaa, the order of the border was sometimes as long as 130 kilometres long, and a 65 - mile [65 km] long queue was quite common. The border order created a traffic hazard and there was also a death. I remember that Matti Vanhanen's government allocated EUR 25 million to build a parking lot in Valimaa to ensure road safety. Our situation was perfectly comparable to that.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U40	Please, my colleague, Mrs Tuiksoo, the second point of clarification.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U41	Thank you very much, Madam President. Prime Minister! By decision of 29 October, the Administrative Court upheld the complaint against the Police and Border Guard Board and declared it unlawful to refer the crossing to an unjustified waiting area before border control operations were carried out. The Court found that it was unlawful to charge a waiting period for border crossings from those who had previously closed the place in waiting order and did not use the waiting area services. The judgment has not been enough, and the situation is still, as you have heard, unnatural and uncomfortable for a border crossing. How do you think the situation at the border crossing points will return to normal?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U42	Thank you. In order of order, the situation has now returned to normal. We no longer have extremely long border queues. I know the Ministry of the Interior is going to challenge the decision of the administrative court. What ends the litigation is very difficult for me to predict. The reservation fee is obviously necessary because the management of the queue requires costs. Our current system is built on the principle that whoever receives the service must also pay for it. The alternative would be for anonymous taxpayers who do not receive the service to pay for the entire service through the state budget. However, it is probably more reasonable to charge a fee to those who use a particular service. If someone has registered in the electronic order of the border crossing and paid for it, when they arrive at the border, I will remind you of the question you asked here before in the Riigikogu, and my opinion is that the registration fee is not to ask him again for any right. In Estonia, you should not charge twice for a service provided only once. The question is whether a charge may be charged for a particular household service or parking space if the border is exceeded. I will answer the following question, and then I will answer that question.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U43	Two locally registered questions can now be submitted. Please, colleague Tarmo Tamm!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U44	Thank you, Madam President. Prime Minister! It's getting weird. I think somebody's very personal interest is at stake here, somebody wants to keep this time from changing, and the waiting period will continue. The Chancellor of Justice has drawn attention to this, the Tartu Administrative Court has made a decision, but the state is trying to keep this dispute with the citizen. It is as if the State would support the legitimate nature of the deferred payment. How do you feel about this situation, Prime Minister?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U45	Thank you for giving me the opportunity to continue to answer the question of Esther Tuiksoo! If the place is closed in the electronic system, the car arrives at the border and there is no queue at the border, virtual or physical, then charging the border crossingr for some non-service provision is out of my question. However, if electronic registration has not taken place, no place has been taken in the queue, and although there is no queue at the time of arrival at the border, I believe that it is appropriate to register the queue for border crossings and also to charge the parking fee. Otherwise, there would be long queues on the roads before the border for those who do not want to use parking services and who ultimately create a dangerous traffic situation and pollute nature. That would cause a lot of problems. Yes, it is somewhat uncomfortable and, in some ways, difficult to understand why those who have not previously registered the border crossing in the electronic system and have not closed the border in order have to do so at the border even if there is no real queue. The thing is, in a virtual environment, in an electronic system, this order exists.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U46	Please, my colleague, Inara Luigas!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U47	Thank you, Mr President. Prime Minister! You rightly said that the idea of creating such a system was that it was necessary to abolish the very long border queues that once existed. For a certain time, the system worked correctly. I made an extract of the border queues in Koidula this morning. The statistics of recent days show that border crossings have been very thin. From 12:00 a.m. to 9:00 a.m., only 14 passenger cars have crossed the border. Are you really saying that these 14 passenger cars are obliged to go through the waiting area and pay some kind of waiting fee? There's no line. It even raises the question of whether they even have to pay the booking fee. Do you agree with me that the system is not working and that it needs to be reviewed?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U48	You probably didn't listen to me very carefully. In answering the question of Esther Tuiksoo, I specifically said that those people who have arrived on the border queue and who have previously registered in the queue system should not have to pay this waiting fee. I have not said that these persons should go through the waiting area and also pay for services they have not received from it. According to the Ministry of the Interior, border management services include keeping the queue for border crossings, which means in particular the creation of software (including hardware is necessary for this purpose), parking in the vehicle waiting area while waiting for border crossings, placing the vehicle at the border crossing point and receiving it at the road border crossing point. Access to the Internet, lighting and surveillance of the waiting area, including video surveillance, and access to the collection and use of municipal waste in the waiting area, are considered as a service. If the crossing is not using these municipal services, I do not think it is appropriate to charge for those services that are not provided.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U49	Thank you. I will conclude on this issue because, when we discuss the next issue, this debate will probably go on.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U50	The next question is put by my colleague, Mr Luigas, to Prime Minister Andrus Ansip. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U51	Thank you, Mr President. Prime Minister! Let me give you a few examples that illustrate the current situation. There have been situations when a person has booked border crossings, but he has reached the waiting area 15 minutes earlier and has then been held under compulsion, the barriers have not been raised and the person has not been able to cross the border before. This does not mean that a person uses a waiting area, but is forced into a waiting area and is forced to wait, even though no car has been registered for border crossings. There have also been cases where the car has travelled directly to the border and the border guard has told the driver that the waiting period has not been paid, and this car has been returned, only in order to be able to pay some kind of fee to a private company. Is such conduct at the border natural and logical?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U52	Thank you. This question has already been asked today, and I have also answered it. In my opinion, it is not appropriate to include a waiting period if no services are actually provided.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U53	Please, Mr Luigas, the first point to be clarified.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U54	Thank you, Prime Minister. We have been talking about this in the Riigikogu for a year and a half. What prevents the Minister of the Interior from amending the law on the state border and coming to the Riigikogu so that we can make these changes?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U55	Thank you. It's a matter of belief or disbelief. For some reason, I do not believe that double-cassing is a mass or standard practice for placing a place in the electronic border queue. You're nodding and saying it's common practice. I can't believe it. If the place is closed electronically and arrives at the border just as the queue arrives, what can be done for the second time? The case you described in your previous question, when the border crossing arrived 15 minutes earlier at the border, was, in my opinion, exactly the time to reach the border. All rules must be humane in particular and, in 15 minutes' time, once again, to be registered in an electronic queue system is, in my opinion, inhumane. That shouldn't be done. I don't blame the law here. I think the law is correct. But when the cases you have referred to have actually taken place, I would argue that they are treated as violations of the law, not as proof that the law is somehow flawed. All vehicles are charged for placing the place in the electronic border queue, the vehicle being a motorbike or a heavy-duty vehicle, EUR 1 and 30 cents. I think it's appropriate to cash that fee. The only alternative would be to cover the costs of keeping records from the state budget.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U56	Please, Mr Luigas, the second point of clarification.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U57	I'm glad to hear that you've realized, too, that you shouldn't take double fees. We have proposed to him that a change be made to the state border law which would prohibit such a breach of law. ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U58	Thank you. Unfortunately, I did not understand what you specifically wanted to change in the law. I have a statement from the Ministry of the Interior. On the contrary, from this statement by the Ministry of the Interior, I understand that a border crossing has the right to charge a queue registration fee only once.

ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U61	Thank you. It is not the case in Estonia that there is one prime minister who uses telephone law and is above the courts or legislator. Absolutely not. If there are differences of opinion in the interpretation of the law, it is reasonable to find out in particular what these differences are about, who interprets the law correctly and who is mistaken, and when clarity has arrived, it is possible to amend the law. When clarity arrives without the assistance of the court, it is the preferred solution from the point of view of the taxpayer's wallet. I very much wish that the differences that have sounded in this Chamber and which the speakers attribute to the Ministry of the Interior could be resolved without the assistance of the Court.

ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U68	The last issue registered is from my colleague, Tõnis Kõiv, to Prime Minister Andrus Ansip. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U69	Thank you, Madam President. Prime Minister! Last week, the National Audit Office presented an overview of the use and disposal of state assets 2012. In 2001 and Alar Karis, the Auditor General, introduced this overview here. Part of this overview is entitled 'Estonian challenges for sustainable development' and deals with the pension system. Of course, it is to be welcomed that the National Audit Office is concerned about the pension system, and the sustainability of the Estonian state, including the pension system, is really important, but the more it is necessary to consider what proposals to make and what not to do. On page 42 of the National Audit Office's review, the colourful letter highlights that pension insurance can be balanced if the average old-age pension is reduced to 200 euros or the social tax is increased by 10%. Although the National Audit Office acknowledges that these proposals are obviously not acceptable to society, they have nevertheless submitted them. What has the government done to ensure that our pension system is sustainable and that pensioners of today and tomorrow should not be afraid of such changes to the proposals of the National Audit Office?
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U70	Thank you. ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U103	Madam President! ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U104	Okay.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U105	Ladies and gentlemen, It's nice to see that there are so many questions before I get to the presentation. But let's get right to the point. I'll give you an overview of the bill bearing the number 490. This is the second reading of the draft State Budget Act 2014. The Government of the Republic has initiated the draft State Budget Act 2014, the first reading of which took place in the Riigikogu's plenary session. a on 23 October 2004. The first reading of the draft was closed, the deadline for submitting amendments was set at 5 p.m. on 30 October. The Commission discussed the draft on 4 and 5 November. During the state budget procedure, the Committee on Monetary Affairs has listened to all the ministers in the field in order to get a detailed overview of the budgets in the areas of government. Between the first and second readings, Urmas Paet, Minister of Foreign Affairs and Helir-Valdor Seeder, Minister of Agriculture, attended the meeting of the Commission on 4 November. On the same day a meeting took place with representatives of Eesti Pank, led by Ardo Hansson, the President of the Bank. As I have said, there were meetings with the rest of the ministers before the first reading. Now I am talking about the topics and questions that were discussed at the meeting with the representatives of Eesti Pank. The Governor of Eesti Pank gave an overview of the outlook of the world, European and Estonian economies and the state of public finances. The euro area's longest economic downturn ended in the second quarter and confidence has grown strongly. The budget deficit is falling in Europe and in other regions as well. Low inflation in the euro area will last for at least a year and monetary policy interest rates will be record low. The bank manager stated that an active monetary policy has been effective. We talked about what is happening in the Estonian economy. However, nominal growth has largely come from price increases. Wage growth has increased and labour cost growth is already felt in prices. Inflation, however, is the lowest in the last three years. Regarding the current situation, it was pointed out that the risk of weakening export demand and the rapid increase in labour costs could also jeopardise Estonia's competitiveness. The overview of public finances and fiscal policy Through the eyes of Eesti Pank, it can be summarised as follows: strong and sustainable fiscal policies, small size and openness require constant preparedness and reserves, and the European Union's structural funds will start to decrease in the future. Eesti Pank will carry out the tasks of the fiscal council provided for in the two-pack and fiscal agreement. Estonia needs a fiscal policy aimed at creating a surplus, and the new state budget supports the achievement of this goal. The overall recommendation is to quickly achieve the objectives of the budgetary strategy and to monitor both structural and nominal indicators. I will now mention some of the questions that were put to the representatives of Eesti Pank in the Commission. They asked about the forecast for next year. The Bank confirmed that their latest forecast is close to 4%. An assessment of tax receipts was also requested. Eesti Pank said that it coincides with the assessment made by the Ministry of Finance. Productivity growth and wage growth were mainly seen as an increase in wages in lower decidals. Foreign Minister Urmas Paet, who went to the Commission on the same day on 4 November, pointed out the following keywords. Wage growth in the ministry is 5% in the middle, which is characteristic of all ministries. There will be high-level conferences in Tallinn next year and Estonia will take the lead in several projects, such as the Baltic Sea Ministerial Meeting, the Baltic Assembly Presidency, etc. Talked about visa. More labour is needed in the representations of the countries to the east of us, and there is also a need to invest in embassies in London and Moscow. Our embassy in Brazil is completely new, it will be dealt with, and embassy buildings in India and China are either completed or are about to be completed. A separate issue was the effectiveness of development aid. Aid must go in the overall picture to combat poverty. There was talk about how to better ensure that help reaches the right goal and how to measure it. These were rules, formulas and internal control in the context of development aid. Minister of Agriculture Helir-Valdor Seeder said that the total budget of the Ministry was EUR 332.4 million, with external aid and co-financing amounting to EUR 283 million. External grants are mainly divided into direct payments and all other international investments. He is concerned that the top-up of additional national support may no longer be paid in 2014. The European Commission has decided that it may be paid by the countries that joined it in 2004 for the next budget period. We don't have that money in the budget. Other national subsidies, such as breeding and development subsidies, are also decreasing, however. The new period of structural resources is certainly difficult for the PRIA. I shall now turn to the amendments. By the deadline for tabling amendments, i.e. By 30 October 2008, 403 amendments were tabled to the draft, two of which were subsequently withdrawn. The Committee on Finance drew up one proposal, which largely consists of technical changes and clarification of the sections within the areas of government of the ministries. With this proposal, § 17 was included in the draft. This proposal has been drawn up on the basis of additional information from ministries and specific clarifications have been added to each amendment. Explanations of the amendments are also included in the list of amendments. Each year, the Committee on Monetary Affairs has encountered difficulties in properly presenting proposals when examining amendments. The amendments to the state budget must comply with the requirements of § 116 of the Constitution, § 20 of the State Budget Act and § 99.102 of the Riigikogu Law on Home and Working Procedures and § 106 of the Riigikogu Act. Now a word of these paragraphs. The Constitution and the State Budget Act state that a proposal to amend the draft State Budget Act, which implies a reduction in revenue, an increase in expenditure or a redistribution of expenditure provided for in the draft law, must be accompanied by financial calculations showing the sources of revenue needed to cover the costs. The State Budget Act adds that the Riigikogu must not delete or reduce expenses arising from foreign contracts provided for by other laws in the draft state budget or ratified by the Riigikogu. The proposal to amend the draft state budget shall not lead to a deficit, an increase or a reduction in the surplus of the state budget. Pursuant to § 100 (1) of the Law on the Rules of Procedure and Homes of the Riigikogu, only amendments which meet the requirements of § 99 (3) of the Act are examined by the Commission. Under this provision, the amendment must include a reference to the part of the draft to be amended and the exact wording of the proposed amendment. Non-compliant amendments may not be included in the list of amendments by the commission. In addition, § 102 (2) of the Rules of Procedure and Home Affairs Act obliges the commission to include amendments which are the same as the author and which are essentially related to each other in the list of amendments as a single amendment. In the light of the above provisions, the Commission must decide whether the amendment is compatible with the Law on the Rules of Procedure and Rules of Procedure of the Riigikogu and the State Budget Act, or whether the proposal may not be discussed and listed in substance on the basis of § 100 of the Home and Working Procedures Act. When making a decision, the Commission must assess the compliance of the source of cover with the conditions laid down in the law, while ensuring that the fundamental rights of members of the Riigikogu are not violated, and be convinced that if the amendments are supported by the Riigikogu, they can be included in the text of the draft. All of this should be borne in mind when proposing amendments, and proposals that comply with the above requirements should be put forward. The Socialist Party Group submitted proposals for a second reading of the draft State Budget Act 2014, including an increase in costs, to which the necessary sources of revenue had been presented as separate amendments, but also reflected in each proposal in a consolidated volume (amendment Nos 3 and 295-301). At its meeting on 5 November, the Finance Committee found that such amendments, which were 45, did not comply with § 99 (3) of the Law on the Rules of Procedure and Rules of Procedure of the Riigikogu or § 20 (1) of the State Budget Act, decided to consider the proposals to be essentially related and to be included in the list of amendments as a single proposal on the basis of § 102 (2) of the Law on the Rules of Procedure of the Riigikogu. The related proposals refer, in particular, to proposals such as those to be taken into account or voted in plenary, which inevitably require that other proposals be taken into account or voted in order to avoid inconsistencies. At its meeting on 11 November, the Commission reviewed the above substantively related amendments on the basis of the arguments of existing laws on the contentiousness of the source for the separate submission of proposals. The Commission made a decision to include these proposals in the list of amendments as separate proposals together with the decision of the leading committee, but referring to the need to amend the specifications and regulation of the annual state budget law in the Riigikogu's Home and Working Procedures Act. The Committee on Finance also examined all other amendments received and listed them. At the same time, the Commission points out that the Centre Party has once again tabled amendments whose compliance with the so-called basic law of the state budget as regards the sources of cover is debatable due to the lack or inaccuracies of financial accounts. Amendments to the draft State Budget Act are regulated by the State Budget Act and the Riigikogu's Home and Working Procedures Act. According to them, the initiator of a proposal to amend the draft state budget law, which implies a reduction in revenue, an increase in expenditure or a redistribution of expenditure, must include financial estimates indicating the sources of revenue needed to cover the costs. The sources of cover provided for in the proposal must be both acceptable and possible. It is permissible to refer to the Riigikogu's pending drafts on this subject or to add an amendment to the tax law to the proposal, as well as to include appropriate discussions on a different forecast of revenue from the state budget. The committee ignored the amendments received from the political groups and members of the Riigikogu, with the exception of one partly supported proposal. Ladies and gentlemen, I will give you an overview of the main reasons for not taking account of this. Firstly, it was intended to reduce the tax gap as a source of cover. The Tax and Customs Board is constantly stepping up its work and is striving to reduce the tax gap, including VAT. One measure reducing the VAT hole is the introduction of an electronic annex to the VAT return planned by the draft Act amending the VAT Act 493. With the effect of reducing the VAT gap, there are also planned restructurings under the Alcohol, Tobacco, Fuel and Electricity Excise Act and the Amendment to the Liquid Fuel Act Act 491. The increase in VAT receipts resulting from these changes has already been taken into account in budget planning, and an even greater reduction in the VAT gap over a year is unlikely to occur. In addition, we ask that specific measures be planned to achieve this objective, but also to present some other sources of cover. At present, opposition parties have not proposed any concrete measures in their amendments to combat tax fraud by the Tax and Customs Board. Secondly, a government reserve is provided as a source of cover. The total cost of the 2013 reserve, together with the surplus from 2012, is EUR 30.38 million. The planned volume for 2014 is EUR 10.09 million, including non-fixed costs of approximately EUR 1.88 million, plus a possible surplus from the previous year. The fixed costs reflect possible costs, reimbursements by the European Commission, by the way, also the purchase of ladder cars to the Ministry of the Interior, as it is not certain whether this cost requirement will be incurred as early as 2014. The reserve of the Government of the Republic has now been used as a cover for a total amount of 6.5 million euros, which is too much. Third, the sale of the land has been offered as a source of cover. Several amendments are covered by the sale of land. In four years, land sales have decreased by an average of 16% per year. The revenue from the sale of land is one-off, and raising the cost level through such revenue will certainly exacerbate the financing problems. The draft budget for 2014 provides for a revenue of EUR 10 million from the sale of land. The reduction compared to last year is 5 million, or 33%. Fourthly, it has also been proposed to reduce the purchase of land as a source of cover for the amendments. Ladies and gentlemen, The State is obliged to purchase land of natural value that is intended to be sold to the State. Due to the number of sales applications and the lack of opportunities available to the State, the queue has already stretched to over six years. The draft 2014 is set at EUR 5 million for the purchase of land. Growth, by the way, is 3.5 million compared to last year, i.e. in percentage terms, quite decent. Fifthly, the sums intended to be used for the construction of the presidential castle border garden were also intended as a source of cover. The budget provides for EUR 403000 for the construction of the border garden of the administrative building of the Office of the President. This is a coercion. The investment must be carried out on the basis of § 2 (1) of Decree No 33 of the Government of the Republic of 19 April 2012 entitled "Personal protection measures for object at high risk of attack'. Sixthly, an increase in income tax is also foreseen as a source of cover. In the case of one of the sources of cover, the Social Democrats proposed a relatively sudden increase in this tax. In essence, it was desirable to apply a 26% tax rate to Estonian citizens earning more than 1100 euros a month. This was expected to generate additional 48 million euros from Estonian workers. As the higher rate of income tax was set by the proposers for the annual salary and did not provide for monthly income tax payments on real wages or so-called advance wages, potential additional income would only arise in 2015, when taxpayers declare their income and it becomes clear whose annual salary exceeds this threshold and should be charged with additional burdens. In 2014, there would be no additional revenue in the state budget, even if this amendment had been accepted. The programme of the Fatherland and Res Publica Union and the Estonian Reform Party Government Association provides for the continuation of a simple, business-friendly uniform personal income tax and excludes the introduction of progressive income tax. If you look at our tax policy as a whole, the principle of Estonia's long-term tax policy is to tax less on earning and more on consumption, which is a tax policy trend in the OECD and also in most EU Member States. This option will have a less negative impact on the interest in working and earning and will also have a lower negative impact on economic growth. In the same way, excise increases have been put on the table among the amendments as a source of cover. The authors of the amendments would like to increase excise duty rates by an average of 20% per year for the next four years in a row. Since there has been no debate on excise rates lately neither in the committee nor in the Chamber of the Riigikogu, and there are different views in political groups, including coalition parties, on the harmonisation of excise rates for strong and thin alcoholic beverages, I will pass on the opinion of the Ministry of Finance as the initiator of the draft, in the interests of regularity. The Ministry of Finance thought that an increase in alcohol duty of 5% per year was already planned for the next three years. According to the Ministry, the exponential increase in excise duty rates does not bring the expected additional revenue to the budget, but may even reduce revenue as a result of the high growth in the secret market. Compared to other European countries, Estonia is already among the relatively high taxing countries of alcohol. But once again, the political groups clearly have different opinions here. The dividends are also intended as a source of cover. The planning of dividend income takes account of the company's real economic performance and solvency, which is why decisions to increase dividends should certainly be discussed with the general meeting, the supervisory board and the management board and the strategic options of the company monitored. Unfortunately, this has not been done when presenting the sources of the amendments. Now I'm talking about an increase in costs. The budget for school lunches is EUR 14.3 million for municipal schools and EUR 2.2 million for vocational schools. Before increasing the school lunch allowance and extending it to upper secondary school, it is certainly worth holding a political debate on how to do this and whether this support should be universal or needs-based. If we chose a needs-based option, it would allow more efficient use of resources and guarantee free school lunch for students who have problems and who are in need. The proposals were to increase the support fund (educational expenditure). It has been agreed with the Alliance of Educational Workers that part of the salary increases must come through a review of the school organisation. Education expenditure is supported by an increase in municipal school expenditure of EUR 9.5 million, and additional resources can be obtained from other education expenditure lines in order to raise wages. School support systems were proposed. Financing the work of logos and psychologists has always been the responsibility of local authorities; they have done so from their own revenues. In 2011, the obligation to provide social pedagogy services was added, the financing of which was provided by the State to local authorities in 2011, 2012 and 2013. The Ministry of Education and Research has considered it more reasonable to develop a system of regional reference centres in the future in order to ensure the availability of this service. It is not appropriate to provide a separate service in every small home school, as there is not enough staff or consumers of the service to do so. The support specialists received 3.4 million euros from the state budget in 2013. The debate led to the financing of recreational education. The 2014 state budget allocates EUR 15.6 million for recreational education and youth work. I mention this sub-section: support for youth education projects, risky children's programme, open youth centres, youth sport programme. The minimum wage for cultural workers was similarly set out separately in the amendments. In addition to the 5.1% increase in the payroll fund, the Ministry of Culture was allocated an additional amount for the 1.5% increase in the payroll fund. Now I'm talking about child benefits. The aim of the current family policy is to increase the necessity of the family benefit system. First and foremost, those who really need it must receive help. In terms of limited resources, we are using taxpayers' money inefficiently by multiplying them. In 2014, the budget for family allowances will be EUR 101,2 million. For information, I also mention the opinion of the Ministry of Social Affairs on this amendment (i.e. the position on the amendment of the State Family Benefits Act): 'The draft provides that the first and second children will receive six times the monthly child benefit, i.e. the child benefit of the first and second children will be increased from EUR 19,18 to EUR 57,45. According to the explanatory memorandum, tripled support for the first and second children would increase the cost of the state budget by EUR 106 million per year. No sources of coverage of the costs necessary to implement the amendment have been proposed. (At least not correctly. (S. S.) The necessary additional resources have not been taken into account in the country's budgetary strategy for the years 2014 to 2017. The effects of the draft have not been analysed. The explanatory memorandum only mentions the fact that 160,000 Estonian families are affected by the amendment. The explanatory memorandum does not include an impact assessment of the reduction in the risk of poverty in families with children and the impact of the draft on specific groups, such as children with one parent, families with children and other families at greater risk of poverty. The cost effectiveness of the measures has also not been analysed. The government plans to carry out an analysis of the family benefit scheme by 2014 and to draw up a concept of benefits and services for families with children. ' When it comes to the death grant, it has to be said that there is no substantive justification why the State should re-pay the funeral allowance to all applicants, without taking into account the economic opportunities of the deceased. Social benefits are more need-based in nature. The amendment would increase not only direct costs but also indirect costs (staff costs, administrative costs, document processing costs, etc.) as the number of grants simply increases. For information, I refer to the opinion of the Ministry of Social Affairs on this amendment (i.e. the position on the draft law amending the State Death Allowance Act): 'The explanatory memorandum to the draft requires the restoration of the payment of public funeral allowance in the form in force before 1 July 2009 for all families whose families are affected by a sad event. The draft therefore provides for the payment of support to all residents irrespective of the person's financial situation. There is no substantive justification in the draft for the State to pay the funeral allowance to all applicants regardless of their economic potential. The Government of the Republic takes the view that social benefits must be more targeted at people who are in need and who would face difficulties without support. It cannot therefore be considered justified to grant funeral allowances to all, without taking into account the economic opportunities of the deceased. ' Public roads of local governments. Taking the proposal into account would make it difficult for a country to fulfil its obligations in the design of a national road network. The state will be able to ensure that its roads are maintained and the necessary investments will only be made in the state budget out of the funds planned. Local authorities have own resources as the main source of road maintenance costs, complemented by state budget support. As a result, the share of support from local authorities cannot increase sharply. The local authorities' own revenue base has grown rapidly in recent years. Only income tax receipts will increase by 1.34 times by 2014, compared to 2010, by 200 million euros, which has also increased the ability of local authorities to deal with road maintenance costs, among other things. Now I am talking about direct payments. The European Union's 2014/20. In the planning of the 2000 programming period, Member States have been given the opportunity to compensate farmers for the reduction in direct payments from additional rural development funds, i.e. external payments. Direct payments to farmers in the 2013 budget amount to a total of EUR 123.6 million, including transitional national aid of EUR 22.4 million, and EUR 110 million in the draft 2014 budget, i.e. the single area payment. Financing of political parties from the state budget is planned to ensure clear, transparent and coherent funding for political parties. The alternative would be to fund them only through private donations. The funding of political parties has not increased in the last four years. Now I'm talking about questions and issues related to volunteer saviors. In 2014, EUR 0.76 million is planned for this activity. In 2012, 1238 people belonged to the voluntary rescue company, and 1122 volunteers with a certificate were rescued. A total of EUR 170000 has been signed with volunteer commandos, with an average of EUR 350 per command. In 2013, the total number of voluntary rescue commandos is 103. In addition to contracts, fixed technical charges, car repair funds, the costs of vaccinating volunteers, etc., must be taken into account. Speaking of student loans, it remains unclear why there is a need for an increase of EUR 8.64 million in student loans, as proposed by the amendment. Growth in the 2014 budget is more than 100% of EUR 4.13 million. In addition, indirect costs (more student creditors, higher interest costs, etc.) would increase, which in 2009, before the legislative amendment, cost approximately EUR 7 million. This is a benefit for civil servants and public sector employees, i.e. essentially a wage supplement, leading to unequal treatment. Now I'm talking about increasing the fine-tuning fund. Municipal tax revenues, revenues from environmental charges and from the Equalisation Fund are expected to reach EUR 945 million in 2014, i.e. 4.9% of GDP. This is 5% more than in 2008. In terms of GDP, collection is higher than before the peak of the boom in 2003-2007. A number of proposals were made concerning the sites of local governments. The proposals to allocate funds to the cultural, educational, environmental and social interests of local authorities must take into account that the financing of local authorities' investments is generally based on an ex ante analysis of actual needs and the capacity of the beneficiary to invest. A quick overview of smaller, so-called local changes. There was a proposal for a division of the operating costs of the Ministry of the Environment. The aim is to reduce the labour and management costs of the Ministry of the Environment by 15% and to channel this money to three sub-offices with an average increase of 6%. A reduction in the Ministry's budget to this extent would undermine the planned actions and objectives. The draft State Budget Act 2014 has a budget of 42.3 million euros (an increase of 18%) for labour and management expenditure in the area of government. Growth is linked to the restructuring of institutions and the 5.1% increase in wages. There is one amendment to the development of Haapsalu Castle. In order to renovate the castle, the government has submitted a proposal to the European Commission to fund it from the European Union's 2014+ resources. The construction of the Bishop Castle should be financed by looking at the castle as a whole, with renovation and development. There is an amendment to the old Võrumaa. Old Võrumaa has been allocated EUR 125279 in 2014, an increase of 10% compared to this year, just over EUR 11000. In the Commission, the question arose as to why this need for growth came from. A proposal has been made for the restoration of sacred houses. By handing out dedicated support to the temples, one could first turn to the National Heritage Board to clarify whether one or the other of the temples is capable of carrying out the work that is primarily necessary, who does the work and who has not already financed the church for the same purpose from the program of restoration of the temples. In the case of the National Heritage Board, it is unclear who will receive the support. The explanation says to the National Heritage Board. In such a case, the amendment is incorrect as it is a public authority. The National Heritage Board itself shares support for the owners of the sites (EUR 600000 in 2014, an increase of EUR 25000 in 2014). In order to distribute money to theatres, the Ministry of Culture has set up a committee to advise the Minister. Ilmaris's support will increase next year, but it will not solve the real problems and problems of the theatre, which are more generally related to the professional theatre in Narva. The local authorities' section of the road can be financed by actions of the European Union in 2014+ (a measure to develop competition in county centres) if this area is a priority for local government. In addition, 10% of all road maintenance costs can be used from the state budget. There was also an amendment to the port of Sviby. Its reconstruction can also be financed from structural funds for the period 2014+ if necessary (first phase was funded in 2007-2013). There were also proposals on animal protection. The draft budget for 2014 for the Ministry of Agriculture envisages allocating EUR 0.98 million for other ad hoc grants. The explanatory memorandum explains that the tools are also provided for information, advice and training activities for the Animal Protection Society and the Animal Protection Union. Within the limits of the funds allocated for support, the Minister for Agriculture will be able to decide which areas need more funding. The renovation and renovation of Pilistvere Church should be financed through the Ministry of Culture, which has set up a separate programme for this purpose. Various other non-profit organisations mentioned in the amendments will be allocated EUR 380000 in 2014 through several ministries, such as the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications. The funding will be implemented through open calls for proposals. When it comes to joining the second higher education institution of the Maritime Academy, it has to be said that it does not have to lead to an increase in costs but, in the long term, to cost savings. Finally, after discussing all these amendments, we took the following decisions. We decided to put the draft on the plenary agenda on 13 November, today (this decision was consensual), to propose that the second reading be stopped (by 6, 2 against, 2 abstentions) and to propose that the deadline for submitting amendments be set at 5 p.m. on 21 November 2013 (this decision was also consensual). I'm positive, and my positivity is based purely on the data that the Troika has passed on over the last few years.

Decisions on disposable incomes, however, are a little risky, because at some point this revenue will disappear and you will no longer have that asset. Last year our position was 52nd place, 47th place in 2010, 37th place in 2009 and 2008, 30th place in 2007 and 29th place in 2006 when the index was calculated. Of our neighbours, for example, Latvia is ranked 12th and Lithuania 28th. The place in Latvia and Lithuania has improved in the last five years, but the place in Estonia has mostly deteriorated. Iceland is at the top of this table for the fifth year in a row, followed again by our neighbours Finland, Norway and Sweden, and the Philippines in the fifth place. Why are we not above this table as close neighbours, but in the middle of the list, in the vicinity of Kyrgyzstan and Russia? One reason certainly is the low political participation of women. According to the political sub-indice, Estonia will be 88th place. See how many women we have here in the Riigikogu, how many in the government, how many in local government councils, municipal and municipal governments? We are still 41st in terms of our participation in economic life. One indicator for this indicator is the wage gap, which is one of Europe's largest, with a total of 30% in Estonia. But when we think, for example, of the number of successful female entrepreneurs and female leaders in large companies, this is also an indicator in itself. According to the Health Area Index, we are 34th and 59th from the level of education. Colleagues may ask why I am talking about all this during the debate on the state budget, how the pay gap and, more generally, the gender gap affect the state budget. The reason is very simple. The state budget is the main instrument through which resources are distributed between social groups. Women and men are the two largest social groups in society whose needs, interests and wishes must be taken into account when drawing up the state budget. Our financial resources are very limited, and that is why it is particularly important that every cent and every euro are spent as efficiently as possible. The use of methods of gender budgeting is very helpful here. As stated in the book 'State budget for women and men. Need-based budgeting in the public sector', budgets are never gender neutral, but reflect and restore gender stereotypes and power distribution in society. Due to the different roles and social status of women and men, budgetary decisions have different implications for women and men. Gender-sensitive budgeting can help to determine how public resources are distributed between women and men. In areas where the needs of men and women differ, the allocation of funds must also vary, i.e. meet needs. If gender inequality is to increase as a result of the budget allocation, the allocation of resources will have to be adjusted so that the national budget promotes gender equality. Several projects and studies both abroad and in Estonia prove that taking into account the principles of gender budgeting at both national and local level has brought clear benefits to the community in the fields of education and health care, reconciling family and working life as well as in many other areas of life. Gender budgeting has already started in the 1990s and sufficient empirical and theoretical material has now appeared on this issue. A book that I already mentioned was also published in Estonian in 2011 and trainings have been organised about it. I believe that quite a number of officials from national institutions are already familiar with the principles and methodology of gender budgeting. So there is a presumption that the budgets of the state budget and local authorities are more women- and men-friendly. However, there is no one necessary component; there is no political will. When we dealt with the state budget for the first time in this Chamber, I asked the Minister for Finance whether his ministry had based its budget on the principles of gender-sensitive budgeting. I replied: "We obviously have better opportunities to increase gender awareness than to draw up a budget on this basis. ' As long as the political elite does not take an interest in gender mainstreaming, we do not move, we do not have more women in politics, we do not reduce the pay gap, more children will not be born, because, as we know, where women have greater opportunities for self-realization and more opportunities to reconcile work and family life, more children will also be born. This means that Estonia will probably be at the same place next year in the World Economic Forum report, but perhaps we will fall further down. Finally, I call on all political parties to think more about equality between women and men, because this is not a marginal issue alongside other major and important issues of society. Gender equality is a fundamental principle that requires consideration in all areas as well as in the state budget. I hope that next year, even on a project basis, budget makers will perhaps start implementing gender-sensitive budgeting. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U373	Eight minutes, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U374	In Estonia, the allowance paid to the first and second children has remained at the same level for ten years. The need to raise the gap so as not to say the ridiculously small EUR 19 child support has been more than obvious for many years. Therefore, in recent years, the Social Democrats have proposed three times in Parliament that child support be tripled. Unfortunately, the government coalition has not taken our drafts seriously and has not taken any other substantive steps. There are only hollow slogans about how important families with children are for Estonia, but no action will follow. There's talk about the need to give extra voice to parents and all that. Two years ago, under the leadership of the Ministry of Social Affairs, the development plan for children and families was completed, which criticises the excessive birth orientation of Estonian politics and highlights the need for universal child support. It is said, among other things, that since family life does not begin or end with the birth of a child, family policies aimed solely at birth and toddlers are not sustainable. The extent to which the state contributes to the well-being of children and families will sooner or later be reflected in the health indicators of the population, educational capacity, the level of crime in society, employment and, ultimately, the overall state of the economy. Studies in Europe confirm that child poverty is lower in countries with significant universal family benefits and parental support services, including labour market participation services. Above all, I am thinking of kindergarten places. Thus, according to studies conducted by the European Commission, the universal system is effective in preventing and alleviating child poverty, especially given the lower level of social protection costs in Estonia compared to other European countries. Nevertheless, the government coalition is only talking about a lack of support for the children of families living in poverty, paying the circa 3000 children a month for just over EUR 9. However, in order to receive this EUR 9 supplement, the family must first fall into poverty. I ask, why does a government with its own people do this to its own people? I strongly support the fact that the state and local governments must help families with economic difficulties much more than they have done so far. However, I am firmly opposed to children's benefits being regarded as part of the subsistence system. If you really want to help those in need, you could start by excluding a small EUR 19 child benefit from income support. With this bill, the Social Democrats have come out of the Riigikogu four times in recent years. Unfortunately, our initiative has not received the approval of the right-wing parties. I would argue that the Estonian government takes the example of the failed southern European countries in family policy, where universal child support is either very small or does not exist and the principle of need-based payment is applied first and foremost. As a result, the birth rate in Greece, Italy and Spain, for example, has been drastically low for some time: on average, over one child is born per woman of childbearing age. There's nothing to be surprised about. If the country only starts to support children when the family has fallen below the poverty line, then children will rather not be given birth than at risk of falling into poverty. On the contrary, our neighbouring countries are acting in the north, where all children are supported. Finland, Sweden, Denmark and Norway have a universal child support system based on the principle that all children are equally important to the state and that the costs of raising each child are the same. The state must make every effort to ensure that children's families are excluded from poverty and are forced to apply for poverty, i.e. income support, through the payment of children's benefits and services to children. This principle has been based on these countries for decades and results in almost twice the number of children per woman compared to those in southern Europe. Our finance and Prime Minister consistently argue that the use of all measures to support the raising of children and the allocation of money from the state budget is irresponsible and will bring the state to the brink of bankruptcy. But this has not happened to the Nordic countries. On the contrary, it is the competitiveness of the economy in the world with the top ten countries, countries where investing in human resources is a priority. In words, we want to be Nordic, but in action we are drifting towards southern Europe. At present, subsidies for children and families represent only 0.6% of the country's GDP. The Nordic countries, which set an example for us, have long realised that the future and success of the country depend on the future generation, they channel more than 3% of GDP into children. When will the leaders of the Estonian state understand this and finally start to contribute, not in words, but also in deeds, to children!? What language do you want to speak, honored coalition ambassadors? Where are your minds and feelings? A permanent confrontation and a tantrum, when it comes to increasing child support, and the gradual linking of child support to family misery mean the next great step on the downward steps of our birth rate and the Estonian people's survival. Dear Members, You have the levers to ensure that Estonian families and state have a future. Use them wisely, forget the coalition agreement and worldview for a moment, and think about the well-being of Estonian families and the sustainable future of the Estonian state! I am more than convinced that, if there is political will, it will be possible to triple child support for the next year. Thank you for your attention.

In view of the fact that electricity prices will also rise in 2014 and this will lead to an increase in the price of all other services, as well as an increase in the minimum wage of 11%, the local government's income base should increase significantly more than planned. I went and studied my favorite Kohtla-Järve budget. The figures show that an increase in the minimum wage of 11% will require at least 10% of the city government to raise the salary of all the employees of the institutions administered by Kohtla-Järve City Government with an additional amount of EUR 1.5 million. It must be allocated to staff costs. Income tax revenue is forecast by the Ministry of Finance to increase by 8,4%, which brings EUR 1.4 million to the city budget of Kohtla-Järve. Therefore, the higher electricity and heat costs will not be covered, not to mention the money needed for development. It has to be said that the pollution charge received by local authorities has decreased from year to year and no funds are foreseen in the draft state budget to compensate for this. The 2014 state budget also reduces the support for local road storage by 4.5%. We consider it unfair that local roads account for 40% of the total length of Estonian roads, but only 10% of the state road money goes there. In fact, money is needed to maintain them twice as much as allocated in 2013. We know that the delegation of the Municipal Associations' Cooperation Council proposed to increase the support for local roads by 56 million. Go on. In the context of the 2014 state budget in the field of general education, it is important that Minister Aaviksoo has promised to raise the salaries of teachers, and this has been discussed for a long time in this Chamber. The Minister promised that the minimum wage for teachers would rise from EUR 715 to EUR 800 per month, i.e. by 12%. However, we can read something else from the state budget: only 5.1% of teachers' salaries are planned to rise. The OECD Education Review also states that unlike other European countries, the share of education expenditure in the Estonian state budget has not been increased. If there is not enough money in education, what will be the cost of teachers' pay? In order to ensure this wage increase, the amount of money in education will be increased and the responsibilities of the local government increased. In the current wage-raising scheme, the share of teachers' training money will decrease to 1%, school repairs will disappear, and any support for activities of interest in school. From now on, the state will only allocate money to the salaries of teachers, to the acquisition of textbooks and to support school lunches. In addition, the state budget does not provide any support for the salaries of school support specialists, nor is there unreasonablely little provision for the salaries of the Director and the head of study. However, this savings also does not provide an opportunity to pay teachers the EUR 800 allowed minimum wage. This will change the basis for all funding: school-based funding will become the basis for local government funding. A lot has been said in this hall about the problem child of local governments, which is, of course, a school lunch. Also in 2014, the allocations for school lunch will remain at the same level as for several years, although food prices have increased considerably. There's been a lot of talk about it. Food money only €0.78 per day does not allow students to offer high-quality food. We believe that this amount should be at least EUR 1. Unfortunately, we cannot support the draft in the form it is presented. Thank you.
