A debate has been held in this Chamber on whether it would be necessary and justified to give additional votes to parents according to the number of children in the elections, in order to better protect children's interests. The party that advocates this principle is currently in coalition and power. What interferes them with better protection of their children's interests? Is the need-based family allowance really the solution? I am thinking in particular of single- and two-child families, because knowingly the birth of a third child is still a prerequisite for having two children. The prognosis that some 30,000 families would benefit from need-based family benefits was not fulfilled. It should also be mentioned that the amount of this assistance per child is EUR 9 59 cents. As of 3 November 2013, only 3250 families, or less than 10% of the forecast, had applied for needs-based family allowance. Last year, during the 2013 budget procedure, I drew attention to the fact that the planned need-based family allowance, which concerns families with one and two children, does not work, together with many of my colleagues from this platform, is not going to produce the expected result. This is because of the small amount of support, but above all because of the degrading way in which it is applied for. The people of Estonia should not be forced into a situation where they must go to prove their poverty and write applications in the local government, depending on the number of children, to receive EUR 9 59 cents or EUR 19 18 cents. Even applicants for income support must write an additional application at the Social Security Office in order to receive needs-based family support. Social workers in local governments, however, currently have a role to play in collecting evidence rather than in preventive and other social work. The Chancellor of Justice has also pointed out in his review that there is, in fact, only one social worker in many municipalities. There is no separate child protection worker and the workload of social workers is already approaching the critical border. The Estonian Social Work Association also acknowledged this at its V Social Work Congress and raised the issue of the heavy workload of social workers and the risk of their burnout. However, I consider the idea of the chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs to be particularly dangerous, so that school food can become needs-based. I think that the right decisions of the re-independence Estonia were to guarantee free school food for students until the ninth grade as well as for VET students. Actually, it should be extended until the end of the 12th grade. This is not only a social policy decision, it is also a question of the state's defence capabilities. Even now, many young people are not qualified for military service because of poor health. Both social scientists and nutritionists have stressed the great importance of warm school meals for the good health of young people. After the war, the Finnish state decided, paying its war debts, to give all schoolchildren a warm meal. That's what's happening right now. They understood that the state was only strong if it helped parents and children not only in a short period, as is the case in Estonia for 1.5 years when parental benefits are paid, but throughout the period of child growth, there are various universal benefits and services. Unfortunately, in Estonia, support for children and families is based on the concept of 'need-based'. Social Democrats don't think that's the right attitude. I draw attention to the large differences in parental benefit payments and the desire to reduce the current income tax rate to 20% in 2015. It still supports more those who serve more, the concept of necessity is forgotten. Finally. If Estonia continues to introduce the principle of necessity in the payment of child and family benefits, young people will choose countries where raising children is safe and support for families is a normal obligation of the state. Our young people leave Estonia. Thank you.

Especially hard to remember, however, has been the pollution that hit the northwest coast in the winter of 2006, which covered a whole 35 km stretch of coast, causing about 20,000 birds to die. A total of 10 tonnes of petroleum products were collected with voluntary aid. In recent years, two accidents have occurred with tankers at Aegna Island. According to Estonia's particular characteristics, the likelihood of emergencies in the Baltic Sea must be considered high, i.e. the likelihood of an accident occurring once in one to ten years. The consequences of the resulting marine pollution are likely to be serious or very serious. And this is where we will never agree with you. As for the whole idea, which applies to school lunch, children's allowances and I do not know what else and which is summed up by the word 'need-based', when we move forward in this way, we will reach the need-based Republic of Estonia. I admit that I have expressed this point here on the floor of the Riigikogu before, but there is nothing to be done, I must repeat it. However, there is no way to agree with all this. The state budget process changed considerably last day. It changed because it was clear to the majority of the Committee on Monetary Affairs before it was a clear minority, so not to say almost everyone it became clear that, because of certain changes in the VAT law and the Income Tax Act, which have not only been done in haste, but also where there is another major problem that this income does not come in, the government has brought a budget of EUR 24 million minus to us. Today, the Chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs expressed his conviction that by Monday all these matters would have been clarified. Knowing the Minister for Finance, however, I do not think he will be able to convince his coalition mates of the need to increase revenue by 24 million, even if he wanted to. At all, I find it illogical that the Riigikogu, without the government, is going to cut the budget by 24 million. That's why I have a simple request. There will be no penalty if the budget is adopted a few weeks or months later. Take him back, make a circle, bring him back to the hall and convince us that there is no empty air in it, and the debate is much more urgent! Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U460	Please, eight minutes!
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U461	Thank you. Please, Kadri Simson! Eight minutes.
ParlaMint-EE_2013-11-13_U462	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, I have received quite a few educational hours here, listening to my colleagues' views on the state budget. Following these speeches, I have come to the clear conviction that the state budget we are discussing today does not have any specific features. This is the budget of one disgusting government, where there is almost no element of novelty. And if it's so casual and normal, do you really have to discuss it for so long? At the same time, perhaps it is extraordinary that the budget presented this year still contains all the errors that we criticised last year, last year and also a year earlier. Perhaps this is another year where the burden is placed first and foremost on those people who are already very heavy. Hidden by recent tax increases and the tax increases that have already occurred years ago, the burden has been placed primarily on people whose income is lower than the average wage in Estonia and on those who are not yet working or no longer working. The budget for next year will continue to give gifts to the wealthiest. If, so far, the gift to the wealthiest has been that our birth rate is decreasing, but the amounts allocated to parental pay are increasing, and that our tax policy considers it right not to collect certain taxes. There is now another gift for the wealthiest: it is likely that stakeholders will also be heard about car taxation. At the same time, however, stakeholders were not heard when funeral and retiring support was abolished. The third negative side of the budget, which is going to send us all next year, largely started four years ago. The cuts during the crisis, which were approved in this Chamber in February 2009 by a single vote, have become a sign of perpetuity. I guess there were a lot of cuts. In particular, they concerned municipalities, i.e. towns and municipalities. The cities and municipalities of Estonia prepared an appeal to the Riigikogu and the Riigikogu Committee on Monetary Affairs. They recalled that, in February 2009, when the income tax part of local government budgets was cut from 11,93% to 11,4%, the municipality's income base decreased. It has been calculated that in 2009-2012 the local living money has been reduced by EUR 185.9 million. This year, some EUR 50 million will be added to the amount taken from the municipalities, and next year, if these cuts are fixed, they will increase by another EUR 52 million. All this is money lost to municipalities and cities, and as a result changes were introduced that municipalities must contribute less to kindergartens and give up youth work at all, as well as support for sports activities. We know that this has not been possible for municipalities. Unfortunately, this "temporary" cut during the crisis will also find its way to next year's budget. In recent years, additional tasks have been assigned to the municipality. And when tasks have been assigned, money should also be allocated to carry them out. We had a serious dispute with the Minister of Education Jaak Aaviksoo a few days ago, namely on Monday, who said that he did not see the reason why the state should fund support staff in schools. Mailis Reps made a long speech on it. Jaak Aaviksoo also said that if the wage fund for logos, school psychologists and social pedagogues were to be given to the State, the income base of the municipality should be further reduced. However, it has been clearly stated in the address of the Cities Association and the Association of Rural Municipalities to the Riigikogu and our Committee on Finance that, if local governments are now deprived of the right to pay salaries to teachers from the salary fund, also for those very necessary staff at the school, the draft state budget will have a gap of 3.38 million euros to cover the costs of support services in the field of education. One amendment from the Centre Party would give that money back. And when we talk about an amount like EUR 3.38 million, we have to bear in mind that the total budget is 8 billion. One important issue for municipalities is the maintenance of roads. We know that 40% of the roads in Estonia belong to the municipalities, but the money they give for their upkeep is only 10% of the excise duty, which is obvious underfunding. Next year, the amount going to local roads will decrease further. These are just a few examples of how the cuts during the crisis are perpetuated, and our disgusting government does not even bother to justify it anymore. If we argue here in the Riigikogu Chamber about changes that we can make in the budget, it is a marginal amount. The amendments proposed by the Centre Party would require a total of EUR 29 million. If it were to be advised to find sources of cover, if it were to be recommended to find money for the wage of support staff and local roads, if it would be suggested that after five years, the prices of food at school should be increased by a third over these five years, then it would be possible to find the necessary source of cover. Just as a source of cover was found overnight for EUR 24 million, which is missing because there is no change in car taxation. It is suitable for taking dividends, it is suitable for selling assets. There is one more important thing that I would like to mention. The Government of the Republic has presented us with a budget based on loans, the introduction of reserves and highly optimistic tax collection forecasts. The opposition of the Riigikogu, however, has tabled only amendments that really, truly have a source of cover. Now, in turn, the IRL and the Reform Party have a very interesting experience of how the Minister of Finance questions the source of cover they offer. I wish the members of the Riigikogu strength in this whole debate, because at some point it must be said that the Riigikogu also has the right to change the budget. The Riigikogu does not only act as a rubber stamp in the swift processing of draft laws that have occurred to ministers. Thank you.
